## `app/ingestion/excel_parser.py`

```python
"""
app/ingestion/excel_parser.py
==============================
FAMILYFIN — CaixaBank Excel Parser

Responsibility:
  Read a CaixaBank .xlsx export file (no traditional header row) and return
  a clean, typed pandas DataFrame with standardised column names ready for
  validation and import.

CaixaBank file structure (24 unnamed columns, data from row 1 onward):
  Col 0  : always NaN                     → dropped
  Col 1  : IBAN full string               → iban_raw
  Col 2  : branch code                    → dropped
  Col 3  : currency (EUR)                 → currency
  Col 4  : operation date  dd/mm/yyyy     → transaction_date
  Col 5  : value date      dd/mm/yyyy     → value_date
  Col 6  : ingreso amount  (NaN if gasto) → _ingreso_raw
  Col 7  : gasto amount    (NaN if ingr.) → _gasto_raw
  Col 8  : positive running balance       → running_balance
  Col 9  : negative running balance       → running_balance
  Col 10 : concepto común code            → concepto_comun
  Col 11 : concepto propio code           → concepto_propio
  Col 12 : always 000000000000            → dropped
  Col 13 : bank reference                 → dropped
  Col 14 : issuer / operation description → issuer_name
  Col 15-22 : complementary details       → concept  (concatenated, stripped)
  Col 23 : NaN / extra                    → dropped

Output columns (standardised):
  transaction_date  : datetime.date
  value_date        : datetime.date | None
  amount            : float  (positive = income, negative = expense)
  running_balance   : float
  concepto_comun    : str | None
  concepto_propio   : str | None
  issuer_name       : str | None
  concept           : str | None  (concat of cols 14-22)
  raw_col14         : str | None  (col 14 exactly as received)
  raw_details       : str | None  (cols 15-22 as received, uncollapsed)
  currency          : str         (always 'EUR' for CaixaBank ES)
  iban_raw          : str | None
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
import re
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Union

import pandas as pd


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Column indices (0-based) in the raw CaixaBank .xlsx
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
_COL_IBAN            = 1
_COL_CURRENCY        = 3
_COL_DATE_OP         = 4
_COL_DATE_VAL        = 5
_COL_INGRESO         = 6
_COL_GASTO           = 7
_COL_SALDO_POSITIVO  = 8
_COL_SALDO_NEGATIVO  = 9
_COL_CONCEPTO_COMUN  = 10
_COL_CONCEPTO_PROPIO = 11
_COL_DETAILS_START   = 14   # issuer + details cols 14-22
_COL_DETAILS_END     = 22   # inclusive

# Row offset handled dynamically by preamble detection in parse_excel()
# _DATA_START_ROW removed — CaixaBank .xls has variable preamble length

# Date format used by CaixaBank
_DATE_FORMAT = "%d/%m/%Y"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def parse_excel(file_path: Union[str, Path]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Read a CaixaBank .xlsx export and return a clean, typed DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    file_path : str or Path
        Path to the .xlsx file.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame with columns:
        transaction_date, value_date, amount, running_balance,
        concepto_comun, concepto_propio, issuer_name, concept,
        raw_col14, currency, iban_raw

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError
        If the file does not exist.
    ValueError
        If the file cannot be read as a valid Excel workbook.
    """
    file_path = Path(file_path)

    if not file_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"Fichero no encontrado: {file_path}"
        )

    # -- Read raw: no header, all columns as object to avoid type coercion --
    # Determine engine based on file extension
    suffix = Path(str(file_path)).suffix.lower().strip()
    if suffix == ".xls":
        _engine = "xlrd"
    elif suffix == ".xlsx":
        _engine = "openpyxl"
    else:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Formato de fichero no soportado: '{suffix}'. "
            "FAMILYFIN acepta ficheros .xls y .xlsx de CaixaBank."
        )

    logger.info(
        "Leyendo fichero '%s' con engine='%s'",
        file_path.name, _engine
    )

    try:
        raw: pd.DataFrame = pd.read_excel(
            file_path,
            header=None,
            dtype=object,
            engine=_engine,        # ← solo una vez
        )
        
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ValueError(
            f"No se puede leer el fichero Excel '{file_path.name}': {exc}"
        ) from exc

    # -- Locate and strip preamble + real header row -----------------------
    # CaixaBank .xls structure (xlrd, header=None):
    #   row 0 → empty
    #   row 1 → "MOVIMIENTOS DESDE..." (title)
    #   row 2 → empty
    #   row 3 → "F. Operación | F. Valor | ..."  ← REAL HEADER
    #   row 4 → first transaction
    #   row N → empty (trailing)
    # We detect all header rows by their operation-date cell
    # (accent-insensitive, handles repeated headers in paginated exports).

    header_row_indices = [
        _i for _i in range(len(raw))
        if _is_header_row(raw.iloc[_i])
    ]
    header_row_idx = header_row_indices[0] if header_row_indices else None

    if header_row_idx is not None:
        logger.info(
            "Preámbulo detectado — cabecera en fila %d "
            "(%d filas de preámbulo eliminadas)",
            header_row_idx, header_row_idx + 1,
        )
        raw = raw.iloc[header_row_idx + 1:].reset_index(drop=True)
        repeated_headers = [
            _i for _i in range(len(raw))
            if _is_header_row(raw.iloc[_i])
        ]
        if repeated_headers:
            logger.info(
                "Eliminando %d cabecera(s) repetida(s) del fichero",
                len(repeated_headers),
            )
            raw = raw.drop(index=repeated_headers).reset_index(drop=True)
    else:
        logger.warning(
            "Cabecera 'F. Operación' no encontrada "
            "— continuando sin eliminar preámbulo. "
            "Valor col[4] fila 0: %s",
            str(raw.iloc[0, 4]) if len(raw) > 0 else "N/A",
        )

    # -- Drop fully empty rows (trailing blank + any mid-file gaps) --------
    raw = raw.dropna(how="all").reset_index(drop=True)

    logger.info("Filas de datos tras limpieza: %d", len(raw))

    data = raw.copy()

    if data.empty:
        raise ValueError(
            "El fichero no contiene filas de datos tras omitir la cabecera."
        )

    # -- Validate minimum column count -------------------------------------
    if data.shape[1] < 23:
        raise ValueError(
            f"El fichero tiene {data.shape[1]} columnas — "
            "se esperan al menos 23. "
            "¿Es un export de CaixaBank con el formato correcto?"
        )

    # -- Extract & clean each field ----------------------------------------
    out = pd.DataFrame()

    out["iban_raw"] = (
        data.iloc[:, _COL_IBAN]
        .astype(str)
        .str.strip()
        .replace("nan", None)
    )

    out["currency"] = (
        data.iloc[:, _COL_CURRENCY]
        .astype(str)
        .str.strip()
        .replace("nan", None)
    )

    out["transaction_date"] = _parse_dates(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_DATE_OP], "Fecha operación (col 4)"
    )
    out["value_date"] = _parse_dates(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_DATE_VAL], "Fecha valor (col 5)",
        required=False
    )

    # Unify ingreso / gasto → signed amount
    # ingreso (col 6): positive float, NaN if not an income row
    # gasto   (col 7): positive float, NaN if not an expense row
    # Rule: amount = +ingreso if ingreso not NaN, else -gasto
    ingreso = _to_numeric(data.iloc[:, _COL_INGRESO], "Ingreso (col 6)")
    gasto   = _to_numeric(data.iloc[:, _COL_GASTO],   "Gasto (col 7)")
    # Money is kept to whole cents so duplicate keys and sums are exact.
    out["amount"] = ingreso.combine_first(-gasto).round(2)

    # CaixaBank exports the running balance in two mutually exclusive
    # columns: "Saldo (+)" and "Saldo (-)".  Negative balances are therefore
    # not a malformed/missing value; they are stored in column 9.
    saldo_positivo = _to_numeric(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_SALDO_POSITIVO], "Saldo (+) (col 8)"
    )
    saldo_negativo = _to_numeric(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_SALDO_NEGATIVO], "Saldo (-) (col 9)"
    )
    out["running_balance"] = saldo_positivo.combine_first(saldo_negativo).round(2)

    out["concepto_comun"] = _clean_str(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_CONCEPTO_COMUN]
    )
    out["concepto_propio"] = _clean_str(
        data.iloc[:, _COL_CONCEPTO_PROPIO]
    )

    # raw_col14: col 14 exactly as received (before stripping)
    out["raw_col14"] = (
        data.iloc[:, 14]
        .astype(str)
        .str.strip()
        .replace("nan", None)
    )

    # issuer_name: extract from col 14 — remove leading "Fecha de operación: DD-MM-YYYY"
    out["issuer_name"] = out["raw_col14"].apply(_extract_issuer)

    # concept: concatenate cols 14-22 (strip, drop empty, join with space)
    concept_cols = data.iloc[
        :, _COL_DETAILS_START : _COL_DETAILS_END + 1
    ]
    out["concept"] = concept_cols.apply(
        _concat_details, axis=1
    )

    # raw_details: complementary cols 15-22 exactly as received (only
    # stripped), kept for traceability before any cleaning.
    out["raw_details"] = data.iloc[
        :, _COL_DETAILS_START + 1 : _COL_DETAILS_END + 1
    ].apply(_raw_details, axis=1)

    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _is_header_row(row: pd.Series) -> bool:
    """Return whether a raw row is a CaixaBank operation header."""
    if len(row) <= _COL_DATE_OP:
        return False
    value = str(row.iloc[_COL_DATE_OP]).strip()
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    normalized = "".join(
        char for char in normalized if not unicodedata.combining(char)
    ).lower()
    return bool(re.match(r"^f\s*\.?\s*operaci", normalized))


def _parse_dates(
    series: pd.Series,
    field_name: str,
    required: bool = True,
) -> pd.Series:
    """
    Parse a date column from a CaixaBank export.

    Handles three input variants produced by different Excel engines:
      - datetime / Timestamp objects  → xlrd converts .xls dates automatically
      - string "dd/mm/yyyy"           → standard CaixaBank text format
      - string "yyyy-mm-dd ..."       → ISO format (edge cases / openpyxl)
      - NaN / None / "nan"            → treated as missing

    Returns a Series of datetime.date objects (or None if not required).
    """
    def _coerce_one(val: object) -> "pd.Timestamp | None":
        """Convert a single cell value to a Timestamp, or NaT on failure."""
        import datetime as _dt

        if val is None or (isinstance(val, float) and pd.isna(val)):
            return pd.NaT

        # Already a date/datetime object (xlrd auto-converts .xls date cells)
        if isinstance(val, (_dt.datetime, _dt.date)):
            return pd.Timestamp(val)

        # Pandas Timestamp
        if isinstance(val, pd.Timestamp):
            return val

        s = str(val).strip()
        if not s or s.lower() in ("nan", "none", "nat"):
            return pd.NaT

        # Try the canonical CaixaBank format first
        for fmt in ("%d/%m/%Y", "%d-%m-%Y", "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S",
                    "%Y-%m-%d", "%d/%m/%y", "%d-%m-%y"):
            try:
                return pd.Timestamp(
                    _dt.datetime.strptime(s[:len(fmt) + 2].strip(), fmt)
                )
            except ValueError:
                continue

        # Last resort — let pandas guess
        try:
            return pd.to_datetime(s, dayfirst=True)
        except Exception:
            return pd.NaT

    parsed = series.apply(_coerce_one)

    if required and parsed.isna().any():
        bad_count = int(parsed.isna().sum())
        # Log which rows are failing to help diagnose edge cases
        bad_vals = series[parsed.isna()].tolist()
        logger.warning(
            "_parse_dates '%s': %d unparseable value(s): %s",
            field_name, bad_count, bad_vals[:5]
        )
        raise ValueError(
            f"Campo '{field_name}': {bad_count} fila(s) con fecha "
            "no válida o formato inesperado (se esperaba dd/mm/yyyy). "
            f"Valores problemáticos: {[str(v) for v in bad_vals[:3]]}"
        )
    return parsed.dt.date


def _to_numeric(series: pd.Series, field_name: str) -> pd.Series:
    """
    Convert a column to float. Treats blank / 'nan' strings as NaN.
    Does NOT raise on NaN — callers decide if NaN is acceptable.
    """
    cleaned = (
        series.astype(str)
        .str.strip()
        .str.replace(",", ".", regex=False)  # handle European decimal comma
        .replace({"nan": None, "": None, "None": None})
    )
    return pd.to_numeric(cleaned, errors="coerce")


def _clean_str(series: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
    """Strip whitespace, collapse internal spaces, return None for blank/nan."""
    return (
        series.astype(str)
        .str.strip()
        .str.replace(r"\s+", " ", regex=True)
        .replace({"nan": None, "": None, "None": None})
    )


_DATE_PREFIX_RE = re.compile(
    r"^Fecha\s+de\s+operaci[oó]n\s*:\s*\d{2}-\d{2}-\d{4}\s*",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


def _extract_issuer(raw: object) -> str | None:
    """
    Extract the issuer name from col 14.
    CaixaBank col 14 has two patterns:
      a) "Fecha de operación: DD-MM-YYYY   ISSUER NAME"
         → strip the date prefix, return stripped issuer name
      b) Plain issuer name with no date prefix
         → return as-is (stripped)
    Returns None if blank/nan.
    """
    if not raw or str(raw).strip() in ("", "nan", "None"):
        return None
    text = str(raw).strip()
    text = _DATE_PREFIX_RE.sub("", text).strip()
    # Collapse multiple internal spaces (CaixaBank pads with spaces)
    text = re.sub(r"\s{2,}", " ", text)
    return text if text else None


def _raw_details(row: pd.Series) -> str | None:
    """Join the non-empty raw detail cells with ' | ' without cleaning them."""
    parts = [
        str(val).strip()
        for val in row
        if str(val).strip() not in ("", "nan", "None")
    ]
    return " | ".join(parts) if parts else None


def _concat_details(row: pd.Series) -> str | None:
    """
    Concatenate detail columns 14-22 into a single concept string.
    Strips each cell, drops empty/nan, joins with single space.
    Returns None if all cells are empty.
    """
    parts = []
    for val in row:
        s = str(val).strip()
        if s and s not in ("nan", "None"):
            # Collapse internal spaces
            s = re.sub(r"\s{2,}", " ", s)
            parts.append(s)
    return " | ".join(parts) if parts else None
```

## `app/ingestion/validator.py`

```python
"""
app/ingestion/validator.py
===========================
FAMILYFIN — Import Validation Layer

Validation rules (in order):
  V-01  DataFrame is not empty.
  V-02  All required output columns are present.
  V-03  transaction_date: no null values.
  V-04  amount: no null values.
  V-05  running_balance: no null values.
  V-06  currency: all rows are 'EUR'.
  V-07  iban_raw: all rows share the same IBAN.
  V-08  amount != 0: zero-amount rows flagged as warnings (not errors).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import List

import pandas as pd


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Custom exceptions / result types
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class ValidationError(Exception):
    """Blocking validation failure. Message shown directly in GUI."""


@dataclass
class ValidationWarning:
    """Non-blocking warning surfaced to the user during import preview."""
    rule:        str
    message:     str
    row_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class ValidationResult:
    """Returned by validate_dataframe() on success."""
    row_count: int
    iban:      str
    warnings:  List[ValidationWarning] = field(default_factory=list)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Required columns
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
_REQUIRED_COLUMNS = [
    "transaction_date",
    "value_date",
    "amount",
    "running_balance",
    "concepto_comun",
    "concepto_propio",
    "issuer_name",
    "concept",
    "raw_col14",
    "currency",
    "iban_raw",
]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def validate_dataframe(df: pd.DataFrame) -> ValidationResult:
    """
    Run all validation rules on a parsed DataFrame.

    Returns
    -------
    ValidationResult on success.

    Raises
    ------
    ValidationError on any blocking failure (V-01 to V-07).
    """
    warnings: List[ValidationWarning] = []

    # V-01 — Not empty
    if df.empty:
        raise ValidationError(
            "El fichero no contiene filas de datos. "
            "Comprueba que el export de CaixaBank no esté vacío."
        )

    # V-02 — Required columns present
    missing_cols = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLUMNS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing_cols:
        raise ValidationError(
            "Columnas requeridas no encontradas tras el parseo: "
            + ", ".join(f"'{c}'" for c in missing_cols)
            + ". Puede indicar un cambio en el formato de export de CaixaBank."
        )

    # V-03 — transaction_date: no nulls
    null_dates = df["transaction_date"].isna()
    if null_dates.any():
        bad_rows = df.index[null_dates].tolist()
        raise ValidationError(
            f"'Fecha operación': {len(bad_rows)} fila(s) sin fecha válida "
            f"(filas: {bad_rows[:5]}{'...' if len(bad_rows) > 5 else ''}). "
            "Comprueba que todas las filas tengan fecha en formato dd/mm/yyyy."
        )

    # V-04 — amount: no nulls
    null_amounts = df["amount"].isna()
    if null_amounts.any():
        bad_rows = df.index[null_amounts].tolist()
        raise ValidationError(
            f"'Importe': {len(bad_rows)} fila(s) sin importe válido "
            f"(filas: {bad_rows[:5]}{'...' if len(bad_rows) > 5 else ''}). "
            "Cada fila debe tener un valor en Ingreso o Gasto."
        )

    # V-05 — running_balance: no nulls
    null_balance = df["running_balance"].isna()
    if null_balance.any():
        bad_rows = df.index[null_balance].tolist()
        raise ValidationError(
            f"'Saldo': {len(bad_rows)} fila(s) sin saldo válido "
            f"(filas: {bad_rows[:5]}{'...' if len(bad_rows) > 5 else ''}). "
            "Todas las filas deben tener un saldo numérico."
        )

    # V-06 — currency: EUR only
    non_eur = df[df["currency"] != "EUR"]
    if not non_eur.empty:
        bad_currencies = non_eur["currency"].unique().tolist()
        raise ValidationError(
            "FAMILYFIN v1 soporta únicamente la divisa EUR. "
            f"Se encontraron: {bad_currencies} en {len(non_eur)} fila(s)."
        )

    # V-07 — iban_raw: single IBAN per file
    unique_ibans = df["iban_raw"].dropna().unique()
    if len(unique_ibans) == 0:
        raise ValidationError(
            "No se encontró ningún IBAN en el fichero. "
            "Comprueba que el export sea de CaixaBank."
        )
    if len(unique_ibans) > 1:
        raise ValidationError(
            f"El fichero contiene {len(unique_ibans)} IBANs distintos: "
            f"{unique_ibans.tolist()}. "
            "FAMILYFIN espera un fichero de una única cuenta."
        )
    detected_iban = str(unique_ibans[0]).strip()

    # V-08 — Zero amounts: warning only
    zero_amounts = df[df["amount"] == 0.0]
    if not zero_amounts.empty:
        warnings.append(ValidationWarning(
            rule="V-08",
            message=(
                f"{len(zero_amounts)} fila(s) con importe = 0,00 €. "
                "Se importarán pero pueden indicar ajustes contables."
            ),
            row_indices=zero_amounts.index.tolist(),
        ))

    return ValidationResult(
        row_count=len(df),
        iban=detected_iban,
        warnings=warnings,
    )
```

## `app/ingestion/duplicate_detector.py`

```python
"""
app/ingestion/duplicate_detector.py
=====================================
FAMILYFIN — Duplicate Detection Logic

Duplicate detection priority rules (from spec):
  D-1  EXACT match on (transaction_date, concepto_comun, amount,
       running_balance) → near-certain duplicate, auto-skip.
       Logged with is_duplicate_flagged=True, duplicate_resolved_by_user=True.

  D-2  Match on (transaction_date, concepto_comun, amount) but DIFFERENT
       running_balance → likely a genuine repeated transaction
       (e.g., two identical payments same day). Do NOT auto-skip.
       Imported as new, NOT flagged.

  D-3  Exact 4-field match on a ZERO-amount row → ambiguous. A 0,00 €
       movement does not change the running balance, so the balance cannot
       tell a re-imported row from a genuine second one. Such rows go to
       needs_review (is_duplicate_flagged=True if imported) and the import
       GUI asks the user; unresolved rows are skipped (conservative).

  D-4  Never silently guess on ambiguous cases — always defer to user.

Output:
  DuplicateCheckResult.auto_skip    : rows to skip (D-1 certain duplicates)
  DuplicateCheckResult.needs_review : rows needing user confirmation (D-3)
  DuplicateCheckResult.clean        : rows safe to import without question
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import date
from typing import List, Set, Tuple

import pandas as pd
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.db.models import Transaction


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Result type
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class DuplicateCheckResult:
    """
    Result of running duplicate detection on an incoming DataFrame.

    Attributes
    ----------
    auto_skip : List[int]
        DataFrame row indices that are certain duplicates (D-1).
        These are SKIPPED automatically — no user prompt.
    needs_review : List[int]
        DataFrame row indices that are possible/ambiguous duplicates (D-3).
        GUI must prompt the user for each.
    clean : List[int]
        DataFrame row indices that are clean — safe to import.
    skip_reasons : dict[int, str]
        Human-readable reason string for each auto_skip index.
    review_reasons : dict[int, str]
        Human-readable reason string for each needs_review index.
    """
    auto_skip:      List[int] = field(default_factory=list)
    needs_review:   List[int] = field(default_factory=list)
    clean:          List[int] = field(default_factory=list)
    skip_reasons:   dict      = field(default_factory=dict)
    review_reasons: dict      = field(default_factory=dict)

    @property
    def total_incoming(self) -> int:
        return len(self.auto_skip) + len(self.needs_review) + len(self.clean)

    @property
    def summary(self) -> str:
        return (
            f"Total: {self.total_incoming} | "
            f"Limpios: {len(self.clean)} | "
            f"Auto-omitidos: {len(self.auto_skip)} | "
            f"Revisión pendiente: {len(self.needs_review)}"
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def check_duplicates(
    incoming: pd.DataFrame,
    session: Session,
    user_id: int,
    account_id: int,
) -> DuplicateCheckResult:
    """
    Check each row of `incoming` against existing transactions in the DB.

    Parameters
    ----------
    incoming : pd.DataFrame
        Output of excel_parser.parse_excel() (already validated).
    session : Session
        Active SQLAlchemy session (read-only during this call).
    user_id : int
        ID of the authenticated user.
    account_id : int
        ID of the target account.

    Returns
    -------
    DuplicateCheckResult
    """
    result = DuplicateCheckResult()

    # -- Build lookup set from existing transactions ----------------------
    # Key for D-1 exact match: (date, concepto_comun, amount, running_balance)
    # Key for D-2/D-3 partial: (date, concepto_comun, amount)
    existing_exact: Set[Tuple] = set()
    existing_partial: Set[Tuple] = set()

    existing_txns = (
        session.query(
            Transaction.transaction_date,
            Transaction.concepto_comun,
            Transaction.amount,
            Transaction.running_balance,
        )
        .filter(
            Transaction.user_id    == user_id,
            Transaction.account_id == account_id,
        )
        .all()
    )

    for txn in existing_txns:
        exact_key   = (txn.transaction_date, txn.concepto_comun,
                       txn.amount, txn.running_balance)
        partial_key = (txn.transaction_date, txn.concepto_comun, txn.amount)
        existing_exact.add(exact_key)
        existing_partial.add(partial_key)

    # -- Check each incoming row -----------------------------------------
    # Track duplicates WITHIN the incoming batch itself (intra-batch)
    # to catch duplicate rows inside the same file.
    intra_batch_exact: Set[Tuple]   = set()
    intra_batch_partial: Set[Tuple] = set()

    for idx, row in incoming.iterrows():
        txn_date    = row["transaction_date"]
        conc_comun  = row.get("concepto_comun")
        amount      = row["amount"]
        balance     = row["running_balance"]

        exact_key   = (txn_date, conc_comun, amount, balance)
        partial_key = (txn_date, conc_comun, amount)

        # D-3: exact match on a zero-amount row → ambiguous, ask the user
        if amount == 0 and (
            exact_key in existing_exact or exact_key in intra_batch_exact
        ):
            result.needs_review.append(idx)
            where = "en BD" if exact_key in existing_exact else "dentro del fichero"
            result.review_reasons[idx] = (
                f"Posible duplicado {where} con importe 0,00 € "
                f"(el saldo no permite distinguirlo): "
                f"{txn_date} | {conc_comun} | Saldo {balance:.2f} €"
            )
            continue

        # D-1: Exact match against DB → auto-skip
        if exact_key in existing_exact:
            result.auto_skip.append(idx)
            result.skip_reasons[idx] = (
                f"Duplicado exacto en BD: "
                f"{txn_date} | {conc_comun} | {amount:+.2f} € | "
                f"Saldo {balance:.2f} €"
            )
            continue

        # D-1 intra-batch: exact match within this same file → auto-skip
        if exact_key in intra_batch_exact:
            result.auto_skip.append(idx)
            result.skip_reasons[idx] = (
                f"Duplicado exacto dentro del fichero: "
                f"{txn_date} | {conc_comun} | {amount:+.2f} € | "
                f"Saldo {balance:.2f} €"
            )
            continue

        # D-2: Partial match (same date + concepto + amount, diff balance)
        # → This is a genuine repeated transaction. Import as clean, no flag.
        # (We do NOT add a needs_review here per spec rule D-2.)

        # D-2: Partial match (same date + concepto + amount, diff balance)
        # → This is a genuine repeated transaction and should be imported
        # as a normal row (clean). No user flagging for intra-batch partial
        # matches per spec. We therefore fall through to the 'clean' branch.

        # Clean row
        result.clean.append(idx)
        intra_batch_exact.add(exact_key)
        intra_batch_partial.add(partial_key)

    return result
```

## `app/ingestion/importer.py`

```python
"""
app/ingestion/importer.py
==========================
FAMILYFIN — Import Orchestrator

Responsibility:
  Orchestrate the full import pipeline:
    1. Parse   : excel_parser.parse_excel()
    2. Validate: validator.validate_dataframe()
    3. Duplicate check: duplicate_detector.check_duplicates()
    4. Persist : write ImportBatch + Transaction rows to DB
    5. Integrity check: trigger balance reconciliation after import

  This module contains NO GUI logic — it returns structured results
  that the GUI (app/gui/import_flow.py) uses to drive dialogs and progress.

Key design decisions:
  - All DB writes happen inside a single transaction per batch.
    On any unhandled exception, the session is rolled back cleanly.
  - User-resolved duplicate decisions are passed in as a dict
    {row_index: bool} where True = "import anyway", False = "skip".
  - TransactionType is derived from amount sign:
      amount > 0 → income
      amount < 0 → expense
      amount == 0 → unknown
  - source_file stores only the filename, not the full path (privacy).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional

import pandas as pd
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.db.models import (
    Account,
    AuditAction,
    AuditLog,
    ImportBatch,
    ImportStatus,
    Transaction,
    TransactionType,
)
from app.ingestion.duplicate_detector import DuplicateCheckResult
from app.ingestion.excel_parser import parse_excel
from app.ingestion.validator import ValidationResult, validate_dataframe

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Result types
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class ImportPreview:
    """
    Returned after parse + validate + duplicate-check, BEFORE any DB writes.
    Used by the GUI to display the import preview dialog.
    """
    df:                pd.DataFrame
    validation_result: ValidationResult
    duplicate_result:  DuplicateCheckResult
    file_path:         Path

    @property
    def clean_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.duplicate_result.clean)

    @property
    def auto_skip_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.duplicate_result.auto_skip)

    @property
    def needs_review_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.duplicate_result.needs_review)


@dataclass
class ImportSummary:
    """
    Returned after DB writes are complete.
    """
    import_batch_id:  int
    rows_imported:    int
    rows_skipped:     int
    rows_errors:      int
    warnings:         List[str]  = field(default_factory=list)
    imported_ids:     List[int]  = field(default_factory=list)
    status:           ImportStatus = ImportStatus.completed


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API — Stage 1: Preview (no DB writes)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def prepare_import(
    file_path:  Path,
    session:    Session,
    user_id:    int,
    account_id: int,
) -> ImportPreview:
    """
    Parse, validate, and check for duplicates WITHOUT writing to the DB.
    Returns an ImportPreview the GUI uses to show the import preview dialog.

    Parameters
    ----------
    file_path  : Path to the .xlsx file.
    session    : Active SQLAlchemy read session.
    user_id    : Authenticated user ID.
    account_id : Target account ID.

    Returns
    -------
    ImportPreview

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError   : If file does not exist.
    ValueError          : If Excel file is unreadable or malformed.
    ValidationError     : If any blocking validation rule fails.
    """
    df                = parse_excel(file_path)
    validation_result = validate_dataframe(df)
    duplicate_result  = _check_duplicates_safe(
        df, session, user_id, account_id
    )

    return ImportPreview(
        df=df,
        validation_result=validation_result,
        duplicate_result=duplicate_result,
        file_path=file_path,
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API — Stage 2: Commit (DB writes)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def commit_import(
    preview:           ImportPreview,
    session:           Session,
    user_id:           int,
    account_id:        int,
    user_decisions:    Dict[int, bool],
) -> ImportSummary:
    """
    Write the approved rows to the DB as a single atomic transaction.

    Parameters
    ----------
    preview        : ImportPreview from prepare_import().
    session        : Active SQLAlchemy session (will be committed here).
    user_id        : Authenticated user ID.
    account_id     : Target account ID.
    user_decisions : {row_index: True/False}
                     True  = user says "import this possible duplicate"
                     False = user says "skip this possible duplicate"
                     Rows in needs_review that are NOT in this dict are
                     skipped by default (conservative).

    Returns
    -------
    ImportSummary
    """
    df               = preview.df
    dup_result       = preview.duplicate_result
    file_path        = preview.file_path
    warnings: List[str] = [w.message for w in preview.validation_result.warnings]

    rows_imported = 0
    rows_skipped  = 0
    rows_errors   = 0
    imported_ids: List[int] = []

    try:
        # -- Create ImportBatch record ------------------------------------
        batch = ImportBatch(
            user_id          = user_id,
            filename         = file_path.name,
            import_timestamp = datetime.now(timezone.utc),
            rows_imported    = 0,
            rows_skipped     = 0,
            status           = ImportStatus.pending,
        )
        session.add(batch)
        session.flush()  # get batch.id before inserting transactions

        # -- Determine which rows to import ------------------------------
        # auto_skip → always skip
        # needs_review → import only if user_decisions[idx] is True
        # clean → always import
        skip_indices = set(dup_result.auto_skip)
        for idx in dup_result.needs_review:
            if not user_decisions.get(idx, False):
                skip_indices.add(idx)

        import_indices = [
            i for i in range(len(df))
            if i not in skip_indices
        ]

        # -- Insert transactions -----------------------------------------
        for idx in import_indices:
            row = df.iloc[idx]
            try:
                txn = _row_to_transaction(
                    row        = row,
                    user_id    = user_id,
                    account_id = account_id,
                    batch_id   = batch.id,
                    source_file= file_path.name,
                    is_flagged = idx in dup_result.needs_review,
                    resolved   = idx in dup_result.needs_review,
                )
                session.add(txn)
                session.flush()   # get txn.id immediately
                imported_ids.append(txn.id)
                rows_imported += 1

                # Audit log entry
                session.add(AuditLog(
                    user_id       = user_id,
                    table_affected= "transactions",
                    record_id     = txn.id,
                    action        = AuditAction.insert,
                    old_value     = None,
                    new_value     = (
                        f"{row['transaction_date']} | "
                        f"{row.get('issuer_name','')} | "
                        f"{row['amount']:+.2f}"
                    ),
                    timestamp     = datetime.now(timezone.utc),
                ))

            except Exception as row_exc:
                logger.warning(
                    "Error al insertar fila %d: %s", idx, row_exc
                )
                rows_errors += 1
                warnings.append(
                    f"Fila {idx}: error al insertar — {row_exc}"
                )

        rows_skipped = len(skip_indices)

        # -- Update batch counters & status ------------------------------
        batch.rows_imported = rows_imported
        batch.rows_skipped  = rows_skipped
        batch.status = (
            ImportStatus.completed
            if rows_errors == 0
            else ImportStatus.partial
        )

        session.commit()
        logger.info(
            "Import completado: batch_id=%d, importadas=%d, "
            "omitidas=%d, errores=%d",
            batch.id, rows_imported, rows_skipped, rows_errors,
        )

        return ImportSummary(
            import_batch_id = batch.id,
            rows_imported   = rows_imported,
            rows_skipped    = rows_skipped,
            rows_errors     = rows_errors,
            warnings        = warnings,
            imported_ids    = imported_ids,
            status          = batch.status,
        )

    except Exception as exc:
        session.rollback()
        logger.error("Import abortado, rollback ejecutado: %s", exc)
        raise


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _check_duplicates_safe(
    df:         pd.DataFrame,
    session:    Session,
    user_id:    int,
    account_id: int,
):
    """Wrap duplicate_detector import to avoid circular import at module level."""
    from app.ingestion.duplicate_detector import check_duplicates
    return check_duplicates(df, session, user_id, account_id)


def _derive_type(amount: float) -> TransactionType:
    """
    Derive TransactionType from the signed amount.
    Investment type cannot be derived from amount alone — it requires
    manual or rule-based categorisation. amount > 0 = income,
    amount < 0 = expense, 0 = unknown.
    """
    if amount > 0:
        return TransactionType.income
    elif amount < 0:
        return TransactionType.expense
    return TransactionType.unknown


def _row_to_transaction(
    row:         pd.Series,
    user_id:     int,
    account_id:  int,
    batch_id:    int,
    source_file: str,
    is_flagged:  bool,
    resolved:    bool,
) -> Transaction:
    """Map one DataFrame row to a Transaction ORM object."""
    return Transaction(
        user_id                    = user_id,
        account_id                 = account_id,
        import_batch_id            = batch_id,
        transaction_date           = row["transaction_date"],
        value_date                 = row.get("value_date"),
        amount                     = float(row["amount"]),
        running_balance            = float(row["running_balance"]),
        type                       = _derive_type(float(row["amount"])),
        concepto_comun             = row.get("concepto_comun"),
        concepto_propio            = row.get("concepto_propio"),
        issuer_name                = row.get("issuer_name"),
        concept                    = row.get("concept"),
        raw_col14                  = row.get("raw_col14"),
        raw_details                = row.get("raw_details"),
        source_file                = source_file,
        is_duplicate_flagged       = is_flagged,
        duplicate_resolved_by_user = resolved,
        category_id                = None,  # assigned by rules engine later
    )
```

## `app/categorization/category_manager.py`

```python
"""
app/categorization/category_manager.py
========================================
FAMILYFIN — Category Manager

Responsibility:
  CRUD operations for the category tree and seed data.

  Category hierarchy (2 levels used in v1):
    Level 1 — type       : GASTO | INGRESO | SERV. BANCARIO | INVERSION
                           Each type node carries a transaction_type field
                           that maps to TransactionType enum.
    Level 2 — category   : e.g. "Supermercados", "Nómina Pedro"
    Level 3 — subcategory: reserved for future use (not seeded in v1)

  Seed data:
    seed_default_categories(session) creates the full category tree from
    Pedro's Excel (Categorías.png) if the DB is empty.

  Rules alignment:
    AutoCategorizationRule.user_id is nullable — NULL means global rule
    (applies to all users). Per-user rules override global rules at the
    same priority level.

Design decisions:
  - No user_id required for reading categories in v1 (single-user app).
  - category_id is always the leaf node (category level) — types are
    structural only and never assigned directly to transactions.
  - get_all_categories() returns a flat list sorted by type → category
    for GUI display.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import List, Optional

from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.db.models import (
    Category,
    CategoryLevel,
    TransactionType,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Seed data — from Pedro's Excel (Categorías.png)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_SEED: list[dict] = [
    # ── GASTO ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    {
        "type_name":        "GASTO",
        "transaction_type": TransactionType.expense,
        "categories": [
            "Teléfono",
            "Luz",
            "Com. Propiertarios (Cuota)",
            "Com. Propiertarios (Gas & ACS)",
            "Agua Tasa Municipal",
            "Agua Consumo",
            "Asistenta Limpieza",
            "Supermercados",
            "Guardería",
            "Apple iCloud",
            "Spotify",
            "Netflix",
            "HBO",
            "Restaurantes & Bares",
            "Compras Hogar",
            "IKEA",
            "Wallapop",
            "Multas",
            "Sklum",
            "Glovo",
            "Peajes",
            "Gasolina",
            "Regalo boda",
            "Amazon",
            "IBI",
            "Compra Vivienda",
            "Reforma Vivienda",
            "Hipoteca",
            "Recibo MyBox - Cuota Agrupada",
            "Gastos Pedro Jr",
            "Ocio, Eventos y Bodas",
            "Parking",
            "Tabaco",
            "Transporte",
        ],
    },
    # ── INGRESO ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    {
        "type_name":        "INGRESO",
        "transaction_type": TransactionType.income,
        "categories": [
            "Nomina Maria",
            "Nomina Pedro",
            "Aporte Comun María",
            "Aporte Comun Pedro",
            "Prestacion Maternidad Maria",
            "Prestacion Paternidad Pedro",
            "Ayuda Cuidado Menor",
            "Compra Vivienda",
            "Regalos Boda",
            "Reforma Vivienda",
            "HBO",
        ],
    },
    # ── SERV. BANCARIO ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
    {
        "type_name":        "SERV. BANCARIO",
        "transaction_type": TransactionType.expense,
        "categories": [
            "Hipoteca",
        ],
    },
    # ── INVERSION ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    {
        "type_name":        "INVERSION",
        "transaction_type": TransactionType.investment,
        "categories": [
            "Tressis",
            "Aporte Broker",
        ],
    },
]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def seed_default_categories(session: Session, user_id: int) -> int:
    """
    Seed the default category tree if no categories exist for this user.

    Parameters
    ----------
    session : Active SQLAlchemy session.
    user_id : User to seed categories for.

    Returns
    -------
    int : Number of category rows created (0 if already seeded).
    """
    created = 0
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)

    for type_block in _SEED:
        type_node = (
            session.query(Category)
            .filter(
                Category.user_id == user_id,
                Category.parent_id.is_(None),
                Category.name == type_block["type_name"],
                Category.level == CategoryLevel.type,
            )
            .first()
        )
        if type_node is None:
            type_node = Category(
                user_id          = user_id,
                parent_id        = None,
                name             = type_block["type_name"],
                level            = CategoryLevel.type,
                transaction_type = type_block["transaction_type"],
                created_at       = now,
            )
            session.add(type_node)
            session.flush()
            created += 1

        for cat_name in type_block["categories"]:
            category_exists = (
                session.query(Category)
                .filter(
                    Category.user_id == user_id,
                    Category.parent_id == type_node.id,
                    Category.name == cat_name,
                    Category.level == CategoryLevel.category,
                )
                .first()
            )
            if category_exists is not None:
                continue
            cat_node = Category(
                user_id          = user_id,
                parent_id        = type_node.id,
                name             = cat_name,
                level            = CategoryLevel.category,
                transaction_type = None,
                created_at       = now,
            )
            session.add(cat_node)
            created += 1

    session.commit()
    logger.info(
        "Seed completado para user_id=%d: %d categorías creadas.",
        user_id, created
    )
    return created


def seed_default_rules(session: Session, user_id: int) -> int:
    """Create the confirmed CaixaBank rules for a user's default categories.

    Rules are personal because category IDs belong to a user.  Existing rules
    with the same category and pattern are preserved, making this safe to run
    again after the default categories have already been loaded.
    """
    from app.categorization.rules_engine import create_rule
    from app.db.models import AutoCategorizationRule, MatchType

    categories = get_all_categories(
        session, user_id, level=CategoryLevel.category
    )
    income_category_names = {
        "Nomina Maria",
        "Ayuda Cuidado Menor",
    }

    def find_category(name: str) -> Optional[Category]:
        matches = [category for category in categories if category.name == name]
        if len(matches) == 1:
            return matches[0]
        desired_type = "INGRESO" if name in income_category_names else "GASTO"
        return next(
            (
                category for category in matches
                if category.parent is not None
                and category.parent.name == desired_type
            ),
            None,
        )
    rule_definitions = [
        ("Teléfono", "VODAFONE"),
        ("Teléfono", "EUSKALTEL"),
        ("Teléfono", "MASMOVIL"),
        ("Luz", "IBERDROLA"),
        ("Com. Propiertarios (Cuota)", "CUOTA COMUNIDAD"),
        ("Com. Propiertarios (Gas & ACS)", "AGUA CALIENTE"),
        ("Com. Propiertarios (Gas & ACS)", "CALEFACCION"),
        ("Spotify", "SPOTIFY"),
        ("Netflix", "NETFLIX"),
        ("HBO", "HBO"),
        ("Amazon", "AMAZON"),
        ("Glovo", "GLOVO"),
        ("Gasolina", "REPSOL"),
        ("Gasolina", "GASOLINERA"),
        ("Peajes", "VIA T"),
        ("Peajes", "AUT. VASCO ARAGON"),
        ("Peajes", "TUNELES DE ARTXAN"),
        ("Tabaco", "ESTANCO"),
        ("Parking", "TELPARK"),
        ("Parking", "PARKING"),
        ("Nomina Maria", "MANPOWER TEAM E.T.T."),
        ("Nomina Maria", "PAGE GROUP SPAIN RECURSOS HUMANOS"),
        ("Ayuda Cuidado Menor", "EUSKO JAURLARITZA-GOBIERNO VASCO"),
        ("Apple iCloud", "APPLE"),
        ("Apple iCloud", "ITUNES"),
        ("Transporte", "UBER"),
        ("Transporte", "RENFE"),
        ("Transporte", "METRO"),
        ("Ocio, Eventos y Bodas", "PADEL"),
    ]

    created = 0
    for category_name, pattern in rule_definitions:
        category = find_category(category_name)
        if category is None:
            raise ValueError(
                f"No existe la categoría por defecto '{category_name}' "
                f"para user_id={user_id}."
            )

        exists = (
            session.query(AutoCategorizationRule)
            .filter(
                AutoCategorizationRule.user_id == user_id,
                AutoCategorizationRule.category_id == category.id,
                AutoCategorizationRule.match_pattern == pattern,
                AutoCategorizationRule.match_type == MatchType.contains,
            )
            .first()
        )
        if exists is not None:
            continue

        create_rule(
            session,
            category_id=category.id,
            match_pattern=pattern,
            match_type=MatchType.contains,
            priority=100,
            user_id=user_id,
        )
        created += 1

    logger.info(
        "Reglas por defecto completadas para user_id=%d: %d creadas.",
        user_id,
        created,
    )
    return created


def get_all_categories(
    session: Session,
    user_id: int,
    level: Optional[CategoryLevel] = None,
) -> List[Category]:
    """
    Return all categories for a user, optionally filtered by level.
    Sorted by: type name → category name.

    Parameters
    ----------
    session : Active SQLAlchemy session.
    user_id : Target user.
    level   : Optional filter (CategoryLevel.type / .category / .subcategory).

    Returns
    -------
    List[Category] sorted alphabetically within each type.
    """
    query = session.query(Category).filter(Category.user_id == user_id)
    if level is not None:
        query = query.filter(Category.level == level)
    return query.order_by(Category.name).all()


def get_category_by_id(
    session: Session,
    category_id: int,
) -> Optional[Category]:
    """Return a Category by primary key, or None if not found."""
    return session.get(Category, category_id)


def create_category(
    session:          Session,
    user_id:          int,
    name:             str,
    level:            CategoryLevel,
    parent_id:        Optional[int]          = None,
    transaction_type: Optional[TransactionType] = None,
) -> Category:
    """
    Create a new category node.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If name is empty, or parent_id is missing for non-type levels.
    """
    if not name or not name.strip():
        raise ValueError("El nombre de la categoría no puede estar vacío.")
    if level != CategoryLevel.type and parent_id is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Las categorías de nivel '{level.value}' requieren un parent_id."
        )

    cat = Category(
        user_id          = user_id,
        parent_id        = parent_id,
        name             = name.strip(),
        level            = level,
        transaction_type = transaction_type,
        created_at       = datetime.now(timezone.utc),
    )
    session.add(cat)
    session.commit()
    session.refresh(cat)
    logger.info(
        "Categoría creada: '%s' (level=%s, id=%d)",
        cat.name, cat.level.value, cat.id
    )
    return cat


def update_category_name(
    session:     Session,
    category_id: int,
    new_name:    str,
) -> Category:
    """
    Rename an existing category.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If category not found or new name is empty.
    """
    if not new_name or not new_name.strip():
        raise ValueError("El nuevo nombre no puede estar vacío.")

    cat = session.get(Category, category_id)
    if cat is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Categoría con id={category_id} no encontrada.")

    cat.name = new_name.strip()
    session.commit()
    session.refresh(cat)
    logger.info("Categoría id=%d renombrada a '%s'.", category_id, cat.name)
    return cat


def delete_category(
    session:     Session,
    category_id: int,
) -> None:
    """
    Delete a category and all its children (cascade).
    Transactions assigned to this category will have category_id set to NULL
    (handled by the DB FK ondelete=SET NULL).

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If category not found.
    """
    cat = session.get(Category, category_id)
    if cat is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Categoría con id={category_id} no encontrada.")

    name = cat.name
    session.delete(cat)
    session.commit()
    logger.info("Categoría '%s' (id=%d) eliminada.", name, category_id)


def assign_transaction_category(
    session:        Session,
    transaction_id: int,
    category_id:    Optional[int],
) -> None:
    """
    Manually assign (or clear) the category of a transaction.

    Parameters
    ----------
    transaction_id : Target transaction.
    category_id    : New category ID, or None to clear.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If transaction not found.
    """
    from app.db.models import CATEGORY_SOURCE_MANUAL, Transaction  # local import to avoid circular

    txn = session.get(Transaction, transaction_id)
    if txn is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Transacción con id={transaction_id} no encontrada.")

    txn.category_id = category_id
    # A manual choice is protected from rule re-runs; clearing the category
    # hands the transaction back to the rules engine.
    txn.category_source = CATEGORY_SOURCE_MANUAL if category_id is not None else None
    session.commit()
    logger.info(
        "Transacción id=%d → category_id=%s (asignación manual).",
        transaction_id, category_id
    )
```

## `app/categorization/rules_engine.py`

```python
"""
app/categorization/rules_engine.py
====================================
FAMILYFIN — Auto-Categorization Rules Engine

Responsibility:
  Apply AutoCategorizationRule entries to a set of transactions,
  assigning category_id automatically based on pattern matching.

Matching logic:
  - Fields checked (OR gate — match in EITHER field triggers the rule):
      * issuer_name
      * concept
  - Match types:
      contains : case-insensitive substring match
      exact    : case-insensitive full-string match
      regex    : Python re.search() match (case-insensitive)
  - Priority: lower number = higher priority (rule with priority=1
    beats priority=100). First match wins.
  - Conflict detection: if two rules of different categories match the
    SAME transaction at DIFFERENT priorities, the higher-priority rule
    wins silently. If two rules match at the SAME priority, a conflict
    is flagged and the user is notified (no auto-assignment).

Global vs. per-user rules:
  - AutoCategorizationRule.user_id = NULL  → global rule (all users)
  - AutoCategorizationRule.user_id = X     → rule for user X only
  - Per-user rules override global rules at the SAME priority.
  - Load order: global rules first, then user rules (user rules can
    shadow global rules at equal priority).
  - Categories belong to one user, so a global rule's category_id points
    into its creator's tree. For any other user the rule is applied to the
    equivalent category in that user's own tree (same name and same parent
    type name); if there is none, the global rule is skipped for that user.

Output:
  RulesEngineResult.assigned   : {transaction_id: category_id}
  RulesEngineResult.conflicts  : {transaction_id: [RuleConflict, ...]}
  RulesEngineResult.unmatched  : [transaction_id, ...]
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Dict, List, Optional

from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.db.models import (
    CATEGORY_SOURCE_MANUAL,
    CATEGORY_SOURCE_RULE,
    AutoCategorizationRule,
    Category,
    MatchType,
    Transaction,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Result types
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class RuleConflict:
    """Two rules matched the same transaction at the same priority."""
    transaction_id: int
    rule_a_id:      int
    rule_b_id:      int
    category_a_id:  int
    category_b_id:  int
    priority:       int
    field_matched:  str   # 'issuer_name' | 'concept' | 'both'
    pattern_a:      str
    pattern_b:      str


@dataclass
class RulesEngineResult:
    """
    Result of running the rules engine over a batch of transactions.

    Attributes
    ----------
    assigned  : dict {transaction_id: category_id} — auto-assigned.
    assigned_by_rule : dict {rule_id: transaction_count} — assignments won
                       by each rule.
    transaction_ids_by_rule : dict {rule_id: [transaction_id, ...]} —
                              transactions assigned by each rule.
    conflicts : dict {transaction_id: List[RuleConflict]} — needs user input.
    unmatched : List[transaction_id] — no rule matched.
    applied   : int — total rules applied (assigned + conflict rows).
    transactions_by_id : dict {transaction_id: Transaction} — the ORM
                          objects already loaded while matching, so callers
                          (e.g. apply_rules_and_commit) don't need to
                          re-fetch them one by one.
    """
    assigned:  Dict[int, int]               = field(default_factory=dict)
    assigned_by_rule: Dict[int, int]        = field(default_factory=dict)
    transaction_ids_by_rule: Dict[int, List[int]] = field(
        default_factory=dict
    )
    conflicts: Dict[int, List[RuleConflict]] = field(default_factory=dict)
    unmatched: List[int]                    = field(default_factory=list)
    transactions_by_id: Dict[int, Transaction] = field(default_factory=dict)

    @property
    def applied(self) -> int:
        return len(self.assigned) + len(self.conflicts)

    @property
    def summary(self) -> str:
        return (
            f"Asignadas: {len(self.assigned)} | "
            f"Conflictos: {len(self.conflicts)} | "
            f"Sin categoría: {len(self.unmatched)}"
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def apply_rules(
    session:        Session,
    transaction_ids: List[int],
    user_id:        int,
) -> RulesEngineResult:
    """
    Apply all applicable rules to the given transaction IDs.

    Rules are loaded ordered by priority ASC (lowest = highest priority).
    Global rules (user_id IS NULL) are loaded first, then user-specific
    rules. At equal priority, user rules shadow global rules.

    Parameters
    ----------
    session         : Active SQLAlchemy session.
    transaction_ids : List of transaction primary keys to process.
    user_id         : Authenticated user (for loading per-user rules).

    Returns
    -------
    RulesEngineResult
    """
    result = RulesEngineResult()

    if not transaction_ids:
        return result

    # -- Load rules (global + user-specific), ordered by priority ASC ------
    rules = _load_rules(session, user_id)
    category_by_rule_id = _resolve_rule_categories(session, rules, user_id)
    rules = [rule for rule in rules if rule.id in category_by_rule_id]
    if not rules:
        logger.info("No hay reglas de auto-categorización definidas.")
        result.unmatched = list(transaction_ids)
        return result

    # -- Load transactions ---------------------------------------------------
    transactions = (
        session.query(Transaction)
        .filter(Transaction.id.in_(transaction_ids))
        .all()
    )

    # -- Precompute each rule's lower-cased pattern once, instead of on
    #    every rule x field x transaction comparison. --------------------
    pattern_lower_by_rule_id = {
        rule.id: rule.match_pattern.lower() for rule in rules
    }

    # -- Apply rules to each transaction ------------------------------------
    for txn in transactions:
        _process_transaction(
            txn, rules, result, pattern_lower_by_rule_id, category_by_rule_id
        )

    logger.info(
        "Rules engine completado: %s", result.summary
    )
    result.transactions_by_id = {txn.id: txn for txn in transactions}
    return result


def apply_rules_and_commit(
    session:         Session,
    transaction_ids: List[int],
    user_id:         int,
    overwrite:       bool = False,
) -> RulesEngineResult:
    """
    Apply rules AND persist the assignments to the DB.

    Parameters
    ----------
    overwrite : If True, re-categorize transactions that already have a
                category_id (used when rules are edited/deleted).
                If False (default), only categorize transactions with
                category_id=NULL (used on first import).
                Categories a user set by hand (category_source='manual')
                are never overwritten, in either mode.

    Conflicted and unmatched transactions are left untouched.
    """
    result = apply_rules(session, transaction_ids, user_id)

    # Write assignments, reusing the Transaction objects apply_rules already
    # loaded instead of re-fetching each one individually.
    for txn_id, cat_id in result.assigned.items():
        txn = result.transactions_by_id.get(txn_id)
        if txn is None or txn.category_source == CATEGORY_SOURCE_MANUAL:
            continue
        if overwrite or txn.category_id is None:
            txn.category_id = cat_id
            txn.category_source = CATEGORY_SOURCE_RULE

    session.commit()
    logger.info(
        "Auto-categorización guardada: %d asignaciones (overwrite=%s).",
        len(result.assigned), overwrite,
    )
    return result


def create_rule(
    session:       Session,
    category_id:   int,
    match_pattern: str,
    match_type:    MatchType  = MatchType.contains,
    priority:      int        = 100,
    user_id:       Optional[int] = None,
) -> AutoCategorizationRule:
    """
    Create and persist a new auto-categorization rule.

    Parameters
    ----------
    session       : Active SQLAlchemy session.
    category_id   : Category to assign when this rule matches.
    match_pattern : Pattern string (substring, exact string, or regex).
    match_type    : contains | exact | regex.
    priority      : Lower = higher priority. Default 100.
    user_id       : None = global rule. User ID = per-user rule.

    Returns
    -------
    AutoCategorizationRule (persisted).

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If match_pattern is empty or regex is invalid.
    """
    if not match_pattern or not match_pattern.strip():
        raise ValueError("El patrón de la regla no puede estar vacío.")

    if match_type == MatchType.regex:
        try:
            re.compile(match_pattern)
        except re.error as exc:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Expresión regular inválida '{match_pattern}': {exc}"
            ) from exc

    rule = AutoCategorizationRule(
        user_id       = user_id,
        category_id   = category_id,
        match_pattern = match_pattern.strip(),
        match_type    = match_type,
        priority      = priority,
    )
    session.add(rule)
    session.commit()
    session.refresh(rule)
    logger.info(
        "Regla creada: id=%d pattern='%s' type=%s priority=%d user=%s",
        rule.id, rule.match_pattern, rule.match_type.value,
        rule.priority, user_id,
    )
    return rule


def delete_rule(session: Session, rule_id: int) -> None:
    """
    Delete an auto-categorization rule by ID.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If rule not found.
    """
    rule = session.get(AutoCategorizationRule, rule_id)
    if rule is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Regla con id={rule_id} no encontrada.")
    session.delete(rule)
    session.commit()
    logger.info("Regla id=%d eliminada.", rule_id)


def update_rule(
    session:       Session,
    rule_id:       int,
    category_id:   Optional[int]  = None,
    match_pattern: Optional[str]  = None,
    match_type:    Optional[MatchType] = None,
    priority:      Optional[int]  = None,
) -> AutoCategorizationRule:
    """
    Update one or more fields of an existing auto-categorization rule.

    Only non-None arguments are applied — omitted arguments keep their
    current value.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError : If rule not found, pattern is empty, or regex is invalid.
    """
    rule = session.get(AutoCategorizationRule, rule_id)
    if rule is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Regla con id={rule_id} no encontrada.")

    if match_pattern is not None:
        if not match_pattern.strip():
            raise ValueError("El patrón de la regla no puede estar vacío.")
        rule.match_pattern = match_pattern.strip()

    if match_type is not None:
        # Validate regex before saving
        if match_type == MatchType.regex:
            effective_pattern = match_pattern.strip() if match_pattern else rule.match_pattern
            try:
                re.compile(effective_pattern)
            except re.error as exc:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Expresión regular inválida '{effective_pattern}': {exc}"
                ) from exc
        rule.match_type = match_type

    if category_id is not None:
        rule.category_id = category_id

    if priority is not None:
        rule.priority = priority

    session.commit()
    session.refresh(rule)
    logger.info(
        "Regla actualizada: id=%d pattern='%s' type=%s priority=%d",
        rule.id, rule.match_pattern, rule.match_type.value, rule.priority,
    )
    return rule


def get_all_rules(
    session: Session,
    user_id: Optional[int] = None,
) -> List[AutoCategorizationRule]:
    """
    Return rules ordered by priority ASC.
    If user_id is provided, returns global rules + rules for that user.
    If user_id is None, returns only global rules.
    """
    query = session.query(AutoCategorizationRule)
    if user_id is not None:
        query = query.filter(
            (AutoCategorizationRule.user_id == None) |  # noqa: E711
            (AutoCategorizationRule.user_id == user_id)
        )
    else:
        query = query.filter(AutoCategorizationRule.user_id == None)  # noqa: E711
    return query.order_by(
        AutoCategorizationRule.priority.asc(),
        AutoCategorizationRule.id.asc(),
    ).all()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _load_rules(
    session: Session,
    user_id: int,
) -> List[AutoCategorizationRule]:
    """Load global + user-specific rules, ordered by priority ASC."""
    return get_all_rules(session, user_id=user_id)


def _resolve_rule_categories(
    session: Session,
    rules:   List[AutoCategorizationRule],
    user_id: int,
) -> Dict[int, int]:
    """Map each applicable rule id to a category id in user_id's own tree.

    Rules whose category already belongs to the user map to it unchanged.
    Global rules pointing at another user's category map to the category
    with the same name under a type node with the same name; rules with no
    equivalent are left out (never assign another user's category).
    """
    category_ids = {rule.category_id for rule in rules}
    categories = {
        cat.id: cat
        for cat in session.query(Category).filter(Category.id.in_(category_ids))
    } if category_ids else {}

    def key(cat: Category) -> tuple:
        parent_name = cat.parent.name if cat.parent is not None else None
        return (parent_name, cat.name, cat.level)

    foreign = [
        cat for cat in categories.values() if cat.user_id != user_id
    ]
    own_by_key: Dict[tuple, int] = {}
    if foreign:
        wanted_names = {cat.name for cat in foreign}
        for cat in (
            session.query(Category)
            .filter(Category.user_id == user_id, Category.name.in_(wanted_names))
        ):
            own_by_key.setdefault(key(cat), cat.id)

    resolved: Dict[int, int] = {}
    for rule in rules:
        cat = categories.get(rule.category_id)
        if cat is None:
            continue
        if cat.user_id == user_id:
            resolved[rule.id] = cat.id
        elif rule.user_id is None and key(cat) in own_by_key:
            resolved[rule.id] = own_by_key[key(cat)]
        else:
            logger.debug(
                "Regla id=%d omitida para user_id=%d: sin categoría equivalente.",
                rule.id, user_id,
            )
    return resolved


def _match(
    rule:        AutoCategorizationRule,
    value:       Optional[str],
    value_lower: Optional[str],
    pattern_lower: str,
) -> bool:
    """
    Test a single (pre-lowercased) field value against a rule's pattern.
    Returns True if the pattern matches, False otherwise.

    `value_lower`/`pattern_lower` are precomputed by the caller (once per
    transaction / once per rule respectively) instead of being lower-cased
    again on every rule x field combination.
    """
    if not value_lower:
        return False

    if rule.match_type == MatchType.contains:
        return pattern_lower in value_lower

    elif rule.match_type == MatchType.exact:
        return value_lower == pattern_lower

    elif rule.match_type == MatchType.regex:
        try:
            return bool(re.search(rule.match_pattern, value, re.IGNORECASE))
        except re.error:
            logger.warning(
                "Regex inválida en regla id=%d: '%s'", rule.id, rule.match_pattern
            )
            return False

    return False


def _match_transaction(
    rule:          AutoCategorizationRule,
    txn:           Transaction,
    issuer_lower:  Optional[str],
    concept_lower: Optional[str],
    pattern_lower: str,
) -> Optional[str]:
    """
    Test a rule against a transaction using OR gate on issuer_name + concept.

    Returns
    -------
    str  : 'issuer_name' | 'concept' | 'both' if matched.
    None : if no match.
    """
    hit_issuer  = _match(rule, txn.issuer_name, issuer_lower, pattern_lower)
    hit_concept = _match(rule, txn.concept, concept_lower, pattern_lower)

    if hit_issuer and hit_concept:
        return "both"
    elif hit_issuer:
        return "issuer_name"
    elif hit_concept:
        return "concept"
    return None


def _process_transaction(
    txn:            Transaction,
    rules:          List[AutoCategorizationRule],
    result:         RulesEngineResult,
    pattern_lower_by_rule_id: Dict[int, str],
    category_by_rule_id: Dict[int, int],
) -> None:
    """
    Apply all rules to a single transaction and update result in-place.

    Logic:
    - Iterate rules in priority order (already sorted ASC).
    - First match at priority P assigns the transaction.
    - If a SECOND rule at the SAME priority P also matches and maps to a
      DIFFERENT category → conflict flagged, no auto-assignment.
    - Once a priority level produces a match (assign or conflict),
      lower-priority rules are ignored.
    """
    best_priority:  Optional[int]  = None
    best_rule:      Optional[AutoCategorizationRule] = None
    best_field:     Optional[str]  = None
    conflict_rules: List[tuple]    = []  # (rule, field_matched)

    issuer_lower  = txn.issuer_name.lower() if txn.issuer_name else None
    concept_lower = txn.concept.lower() if txn.concept else None

    for rule in rules:
        field_hit = _match_transaction(
            rule, txn, issuer_lower, concept_lower,
            pattern_lower_by_rule_id[rule.id],
        )
        if field_hit is None:
            continue

        if best_priority is None:
            # First match
            best_priority = rule.priority
            best_rule     = rule
            best_field    = field_hit
            conflict_rules = [(rule, field_hit)]

        elif rule.priority == best_priority:
            # Same priority level — potential conflict
            if category_by_rule_id[rule.id] != category_by_rule_id[best_rule.id]:
                conflict_rules.append((rule, field_hit))

        else:
            # Lower priority (higher number) — stop searching
            break

    if not conflict_rules:
        # No match at all
        result.unmatched.append(txn.id)

    elif len(conflict_rules) == 1:
        # Single winner — assign
        result.assigned[txn.id] = category_by_rule_id[best_rule.id]
        result.assigned_by_rule[best_rule.id] = (
            result.assigned_by_rule.get(best_rule.id, 0) + 1
        )
        result.transaction_ids_by_rule.setdefault(best_rule.id, []).append(
            txn.id
        )

    else:
        # Multiple rules at same priority, different categories → conflict
        conflicts = []
        first_rule, first_field = conflict_rules[0]
        for other_rule, other_field in conflict_rules[1:]:
            conflicts.append(RuleConflict(
                transaction_id = txn.id,
                rule_a_id      = first_rule.id,
                rule_b_id      = other_rule.id,
                category_a_id  = category_by_rule_id[first_rule.id],
                category_b_id  = category_by_rule_id[other_rule.id],
                priority       = best_priority,
                field_matched  = other_field,
                pattern_a      = first_rule.match_pattern,
                pattern_b      = other_rule.match_pattern,
            ))
        result.conflicts[txn.id] = conflicts
        logger.warning(
            "Conflicto de reglas para transacción id=%d: "
            "%d reglas coinciden en prioridad=%d",
            txn.id, len(conflict_rules), best_priority,
        )
```
